WARRINGTON WORKINGMEN'S ANGLING ASSOCIATION.

(N.A. Centre.)

The slump after the holidays was felt locally last week end. The only item of interest was the match for the shield, medal, and other prizes from the Bay Horse Society. There is little doubt that the holiday affected entries here considerably. The set board at headquarters has undergone quite a change lately, and although match fishing is taking premier place at present, there are a few members who are looking after the specimens. For instance, the Gowy seems to have been neglected lately, especially during the Walking Day holiday. We might suggest it was the unsettled state of the weather. However, it is taking aback seat at present, and in previous years the majority of sets have come from this favourite haunt of Warringtonians. Norton has again failed to show the angler it ,is an ideal spot for roaching, although one of 7oz. 6drs. need not scare members for the three specials that are on for this water. All interest was Hartford way last week, and it was predicted on Saturday evening that 2111. would not win a contest. al Sunday there was quite a change, and the winner of the Bay Horse match was found in Mr. F. Jolley, with six fish weighing 11b. sozs. 2drs. These must be considered fair roach for match fishing, averaging 3.10 z. each. The second, Mr. J. Barry, had twelve fish; Mr. J. Green was third with five fish. We think there should be a prize included for members who catch the most fish in a contest and return them to the waters, although we are aware of the fact that it requires a skilful angler to win a contest with, say six fish, when there are others with twelve.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0210.txt | included=true | included =====
THE MATCH.

A start was made from the Bay Horse soon after 8 a.m., and after a pleasant journey, Hartford was reached in ample time for the match. Bain came as we were going down to the river, and although it ceased when the contest began, we were not to be favoured for long as we had a fair soaking. It cleared for the weighing in and memberg did not seem to trouble much. All credit must be liven to Messrs. J. Warburton, J. Potter and committee for the way they got members started off and the conducting of the match throughout. N. A. OPENING MATCH. When these lines appear in print the committees of all the N.A. in Warrington will have made arrange- ments for their opening match. This is the tlrst con- test of affiliated clubs all competing in the one match held in Warrington. A record entry is anticipated, as all the societies are offering a handsome lot of prizes. The match takes place at Newbridge, where every angler has a fair chance, because there is very little broken bank in this length and two hundred could fish the length. So there will be very little trouble for the peggera out.

WITH THE WOODS.

Semi-Finals of the Lancashire Club

Championship.

RYLANDS' SURPRISE WIN.

The great event in bowling circles last Saturday was the completion of the semi-finals of the Lancashire Club Championship, and Warringtoniana will be delighted with the result, which leaves Rylands' Recs. and Wigan Subscription to do battle in the final. It speaks well for the good old town that we had two teams—Rylands and Cemetery—in the penultimate stage of this great competition, and although one hardly anticipated finding the local team in the final, Warrington has every reason to be proud of the result last Saturday. The Cemetety men were in no way disgraced by Wigan's triumph over !hem, for the latter are a tough set to tackle. The situation is now exactly on all fours with that created last year, when Wigan qualified to meet an anparently weaker team and failed: The hope of all local trundlers is that 1913 will see history repeated and Rylands declared the winners.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0216.txt | included=true | included =====
HOW RYLANDS WON.

Rylands met Roebuck at the TrafTi rd Hotel and there was a large number of spectators, the competing clubs being looked upon as representing much of the best amateur bowling ability of the county. The general expectation was that Roebuck would be easy winners, but the wireworkers rose to the occasion magnificently, and by winning—although only by one point created the greatest surprise of the season. Throughout, it was a battle of giants, a game in which experience and skill, on the one hand, were fitted against a wonderful enthusiasm and dogged deter- mination.

llylands knew they were opposed to one of the greatest teams bowling history has yet produced, a team wherein each man has proved ability beyond the average, and because they were undaunted by the magnitude of the task which faced them, the Warrington men must be described as a great team.

There were moments when the Roebuck team were in the ascendency, but Rylands never lost heart, and in the end their determination won-the day. The first game they lost, the second was won, and the two following games being divided, the Warringtonians claimed a lead of four points.

The wins of the Warrington side were much more meritorious than those of their opponents, for their successes included the defeats of the Lancashire County champion by S. Massey and J. M. Nicol by W. Langley, the former dropping one point and the latter six. It was due to some fine bowling on the part of G. P. Carson that the lead was turned to the advantage of Roebuck, though this was only a single. The advantage was quickly lost, however, as the result of a victory for J. Gleave, the popular Ryland.' player, who had little difficulty in defeating W. Buckley 21-14 in 27 ends. At the 23rd end Weave had had a lead of 21-11, but Buckley managed to add three sing/es before the final wood was soot down.

The Roebuck captain, J. W. G. Coombs, who is also the County president,recorded another win by defeating W. Cottatn 21-18 ; and his example being followed by A. Bennett, everyone regarded Roebuck's victory as assured. The best part of the match, however, was seen in the last games. E. W. Smith and W. W. Wells both had to knuckle under to W. Roughley and J. Langley, Smith and Roughley put up the best game of the match. After Wells had been beaten 2.1-17 in 28 ends by Langley, Roebuck had a lead of six points.

It was on the game between Smith and Roughley that thu whole match depended, and the excitement ran high. At the 24th end Roughley led IS-14. It was Smith's block and he took his opponent on a fairly short length, where, however, the Rylands' man landed two. Taking Smith back on a similar length, the first wood of Roughley's landed about nine inches within the jack. Smith did not attempt to strike, but played in a true sportsmanlike fashion, and tried to best the first wood by merit alone. Hot Never, his wood landed about a yard and a half behind the block. The next delivery of k oughley saw him make a fair attempt to guard the block, and all but succeeded. Again Smith played in a fashion worthy of the highest sportsman, but failed to take the block, after a magnificent effort, by less than an inch. Had he done so the Flixton team would have entered the final, for there was no doubt he would have counted two had he struck the jack. Roughley had won by 21-14 in 26 ends, giving Rylands the match.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0217.txt | included=true | included =====
This most thrilling finish portends a tremendous struggle between Rylands and the Wigan contingent.

CEMETERY MEN DEFEATED.

It was expected that Wigan would be in the final, for they are a more experienced set of players than the Cemetery men, who, however, put up a plucky fight, and are deserving of every congratulation for getting so near the final. Both teams gave an excellent display on a notoriously difficult green, and the bowling was well worth watching, good woods bping frequently beaten on "marks," where the slightest error in direction resulted in the bowl finish- ing yards away from the jack. The green was in splendid order and the running was perfect.

Warrington had the first scalp to their girdle, T. N. Fielding leading throughout with 4-0, 93, and 15-7 against J. Dickinson. Fielding later reached 18-9, and after two singles to his ofponent, made game at the twenty-fourth end. The next game was also a short one, Wigan this time supplying the winner in J. Fairhurst, who played a groat garne,and always had the measure of W. Barton. Fairhurst romped away to 12-2 and 10-4, and after Barton had added a double and a single, was a winner by 14 points in a game of it) ends.

The best game of the afternoon was that in which G. Ball, jun., lost to H. Dickenson by a single after some very exciting ends had been run. The Warring- ton bowler was behind at 4-8, but then scored in five consecutive ends and led 10-8, and later 12-9. The players "peeled " atl2, Ball subsequently leading at 17-12 and 19-14. Dickinson then reached 18-19, and the players were again level at 20, the Wigan bowler finally beating a good wood at the thirty-fourth end. G. Ashcroft, the county player, appeared likely to return a big win for Wigan at one juncture against W. Butterworth. After being in arrears 2-8, 5-11 and 7-19 the Warrington trundler played a doggedly determined game and reached 12-19. At this point Ashcroft had a chance of finishing the game, but was only equal to a single, and Butterworth went on to add 3 to his score ere a bad lead lost him the game at the thirty first end. The game between 3. Downes and W. Bridge was only a short one. At the 13th end Bridge was cnly 9-10 behind. From this point Downes was in great form, and with four doubles and three singles was an easy winner by 12 points. W. Worthington recorded the fifth successive win for Wigan, being well ahead of C. Smith at 14 4 and 19-7. The Warrington trundler then notched four singles and a double before Worthington settled matters at the twenty-sixth end. Wigan had claimed five victors out of six, and established a substantial lead of 31 points when the match was half complet3d.

The monotony was varied when A. Foster returned a winning card for Warrington, beating F. Bullen, the one time famous runner, by 10 points at the 24th end. In a keen game of 27 ends, J. Ashton and W. Topping peeled five times at 7, 12, 14., 10 and 18. With a double and a single, Ashton recorded Warrington's third victory.

The Wigan men's lead was reduced to ono of IS points after the seventh and eighth games. Their supporters felt somewhat uneasy, for at this stage IL Antrebus was well in front of J. Bridge. At the outset Antrobus led 8-3, and, playing a grand game for his side, reached 16-3. After the game had been called 17-0 in his favour, the Cemetery bowler, by the aid of four singles, went to game at the twenty-first end. By really clever play Antrobus thus gained 12 points for his aide at a critical time, and the War- rington club had now visions of victory. They received a crude shock, when in the next game they were practically put out of the running, P. Flynn gaining an easy win over T. Harrison.

Early in the game Flynn led 6-1, and although Harrison peeled at seven the. Wigan trundler was always the better player, and after leading 14-S. and 17-9 finally won at the 26th end by 10 points.

Wigan's prospects were still further enhanced by the two remaining games. T. Ball was in front of W. J. Woods at 8-2 and 13-9, and though his opponent

had a chance at 12-13 he merely added a single to his figures, to retire at the 24th end beaten by eight points. In the last game with John Hart, T. Quarrie at times was very unlucky. Hart led at the ninth end 8-4, and later 15-9, finally winning by 9 points in a game of 24 ends. Wigan Subscription won eight of the games, and the match by 33 points. The locals had hard luck, and we only hope they may be more foitunate on some future occasion.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0218.txt | included=true | included =====
SECOND TEAM CHAMPIONSHIP.

Rylands' second team had to knuckle under to Radcliffe Withins at Belmcnt, although only by one point. Some excellent games were witnessed, and one was rather sorry to see the wireworkers defeated. The games were highly interesting, each player striving his hardest to account for the oownfall of his opponent Each side led in turn, although never by any large majority, and Rylands had hard lines not to win.

LOCAL COMPETITIONS.

In the semi-final for the club championship in con- nection with the Warrington and District Association the Senior Conservatives had little difficulty in dis- posing of Crosfield's No. 2 by 106 points on the Sankey- street green. C. Millington was the only soapery man to record a win, and he defeated A. Scott, after an exciting game, by 21-19, in 27 ends. In the other games the Conservatives had matters practically all their own way, and have every reason to be proud of their great win.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0219.txt | included=true | included =====
LIBERAL CLUB DRAW.

In another column we publish the draw for the annual handicap in connection with the Warrington Liberal Association.

The preliminary round will be played to-day on the Bowling Green at Babk Quay.

WORKS ASSOCIATION. Four games in the fourth round of thi Fiolayson Cup competition will be played to-day as follows:— P. and K. Wheel Shop v. R. A. Naylor's. Rylands' No. 1 v. Richmond's, Academy-street. Howley Tannery v. St. Helens Cable No. I. Crosfield's No. 1 v. County Asylum.

Referee, Mi. F. bean.

The semi-final will be played on July 26th, and the final on August 9th.

The annual main will commence on August 16th. It has been decided to play the handicap in sections as follows :—To draw 128 names (64 games) for each Saturday. After the completion of these 64 games the winners are drawn again and play off; also the 32 winners of these games are drawn and play off; the 16 winners of these games will be carried forward to the final Saturday, when all the winners for each Saturday will be drawn and play off for the premier prizes. By adopting this course it will only be necessary for a competitor to be at the Horse and Jockey, say, two Saturdays to win the handicap cutright. Any player finding it impossible to play on any date must notify his representative on the council of such date when entering the competition, as no arrangements can be made after the draw.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0220.txt | included=true | included =====
THE "HAROLD SMITH " CUP.

The "Harold Smith" Challenge Cup competition was brought to a successful conclusion last Saturday, on the Brooklands hotel green, which was in excellent condition. In the semi-tinal Bewsey No. 1 had little difficulty in beating Latchford No. 1 by 20 points. Orford No. 2 had a far more difficult thing on, for they were only victorious over Bewsey No. 2 by two points. In the final Bewsey No. 1 just managed to beat the Orforditea by a couple of points. The competition has been conducted in a very sportsmanlike manner, and all will congratulate the Bewsey team on winning the cup.

CONSTITUTION LODGE OF ODDFELLOWS.

The annual bowling handicap of the Constitution Lodge of the Independent Order of Oddfellows will take place this afternoon on the King and Queen green, commencing at 2.15. Time limit of 15 minutes will be allowed on the preliminary round only. The following is the draw for the preliminary round :

LIBERAL LAND CAMPAIGN. THE ENGLISH LEAGUE'S HOPES.

Mr. F. Neilson, M.P.' the president, at the annual meeting of the English League for the Taxation of Land Values, held on Wednesday evening, at the Essex Hall, London, said he believed that the dilatoriness on the part of the Liberal organibstions to launch the land campaign had brought the people to such a point that when it was launched it would surprise all the Liberal organisers and Mr. Lloyd George himself to see how it went. (Cheers.) They could look forward with great hope to the future. It would only be a few months now when the land valuation would be complete in this country. Then they would have their great basis to work .upon, and once they had that basis there ought to be no lookinz back. (Cheers.) . . . . .

_ Mr. Albert Smith, M.P., said, the valuation which was taking place at the present time was a great thing to look forward to. When the results were published for the country's use he was convinced they would stagger almost everybody in England.

PUBLIC AMUSEMENTS.

ROYAL COURT THEATRE.

A weird and highly imaginative melodrama entitled "In a Man's Power" is presented at the Royal Court Theatre this week. It deals with trials and tribula- tions of one Cynthia Templeton, who falls a victim to the spell of a wily mesmerist with the picturerque name of Monti Gudala, and is made to obey his decrees, which are generally fraught with unpleasant con- sequences. We also hear much of the vengeance of Buddha and the working of many mysterious charms. Miss Mary Lovett gives a sympathetic portrayal .of Cynthia, her acting in a tt ying role having an intensity of feeling which makes a successful appeal. Mr. Charles Burden is realistic as Gudala, the hypnotist, whose evil powers shape the course of the story. The play affords an opening for the reappearance of that t.iichc.iol boys' hero, Nick Carter, of many disguises, who is impersonated in breezy btyle by Mr. J. Forbes Knowles.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0242.txt | included=true | included =====
PALACE AND HIPPODROME.

Apart from the fact that he bad the honour cf appearing before the King at the command perform- ance at Knowsley Hall on July 7th, George Formby 's reputation as the best Lancashire comedian on the stage stands him in good stead, and exceedingly 1 .rge houses have been the rule during the week at the Hippodrome. Formby's inimitable style compels one to laugh, and he has made a good choice from his extensive repertoire. "They're all gord lads" was as popular as ever on Monday night, and his" Spanish Onion " number brought the house down. He was also decidedly successful in the song "We all went marching in." The conductor comes in for a fair 1, share of Formby's by-talk, and the audience laughed loud and long over most of the gags. Garadini is an artist to his finger tips. As a Protean actor-musician he. is. in his right sphere, being an expressive actor. His impressions of Paderewski ; a child vocalist ; an old street musician and an entertainer are exceedingly clever, and realistic, and the audience fully appreciated the turns. Pete and Minnie Lee convulse the house in their great Scotch comedy "The Scotchman's visit to London." High comedy, singing and acting are introduced, and the turn is very successful. Marie Pascal introduces many new features in a foot equilibristic act, her feats being accomplished as she lies on a pedestal six feet above the stage. Renie Ray poses beautifully in a series of artistic tableaux, and Ellis Barrietcrn is a pleasing vocalit in refined chorus songs. "A queer elope- ment is a highly diverting film shown on the cinema.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0243.txt | included=true | included =====
RAG-TIME FINALISTS.

The rag-time singing competition proved highly successful last week, and Mr. Turpin is to be heartily congratulated on bringing to such a happy conclusion one of the best and most popular music-hall competi- tions ever conducted in the town. The finals were dexided on Saturday night, the spacious building being crowded. In the ladies' section the first and second prizes were divided between Miss Stewart and Miss Merritt, both of Warrington. In the men's section Mr. Shaw and Mr. Campbell, of Earlestown, were declared winners of he first and second prizes, respectively. Mr. Turpin, who was warmly received, presented the prizes and thanked the audience for the way in which they had appreciated the competition.

THEATRE ROYAL.

The picture season at the popular Scotland-road house is pro‘ ing very popular and large houses are the rule nightly. Mr. Morton is to be congratulated on securing such up-to-date, interesting educational, amusing and dramatic films, all of the best producers. "Secret Possessions "and " Dead man's secret" are two very fine pictures, which will be shown to-night in additicn to numerous other films. "The Seeker" was a very popular three-part picture, shown in the early portion of the week,and could not fail to grip the audience.

===== 0002601_19130719_art0247.txt | included=true | included =====
THE PICTUREDROME.

A magnificent sPries of pictures cf the Royal visit to Lancashire has been exhibited this week. At the invitation of the manager (Mr. Cocper), the Mayor and Mayoress and numerous members of the Corporation attended a special matinee at 12-15 on Monday, and were delighted with the pictorial record of a memor- able week. First, an admirable film of the Warring- ton Walking Day, showing both the Church of England and Roman Catholic pageants, was projected. Next came the principal events of the visit of the King and Queen to Warrington. There was a most impressive picture of the scene in Bridge-street, showing the Royal procession passing from tne Midge to Market Gate, between cheering throngs. The presentations at the Town Hall and the picturesque welcome from 10,000 school children were shown with remarkable clearness, and later their Majesties were seen bidding farewell to the directors of Messis. Joseph Crosfield and Sons, Ltd., after their inspection of the works. Of the other views of the Lancashire tour, ,he most brilliant were undoubtedly those which depicted the wonderful scenes in Liverpool. The display by the children in Goodison Park was a perfect triumph uf orwini-ation; 7.1:1Ye thQ review of ve.Fsels in the Mersey was One of the finest maratine pageants ever witnessed. Incidents at Knowsley Hall and at several of the industrial centres visited by their Majesties made up a series of pictures which has probably never been excelled, even at the Pieturedrome. NEXT WEEK.

The chief attraction for Monday, Tuesday and Wed- nesday will be" Oil on troubled waters, " a tale of love and adventure. For Thursday, Friday and t•aturday, " Margaret Catchpole," a charming love story, will occupy premier place.

Andrews' Picture Palace.

A splendid, exclusive attraction has been secured by the manager (Mr. Frank Gallagher), of Andrews' Picture Palace, Br;dge Foot, in the shape of the great detective film, entitled "In the bands of the con- spirators." It is a remarkably realistic drama, illustrating the exploits of a lady detective, and takes an hour to show. There will be matinees on Monday, Wedneeday, Thursday and Saturday at 2 30.

RUNCORN RURAL DISTRICT COUNCIL. Stockton Heath Street Cleaning. The fiatnightly meeting of the Runcorn Rural District Council was held at Dutton on Tuesday, Mr. C. E. Linaker presiding. HEALTH OF THE DISTRICT. Dr. Adams, medical officer of health, reported two cases of diphtheria, one from Stockton death and one irom Grappenhall, arid one case of scarlet fever at Stockton Heath. There was only one case of diphtheria in the hospitil.. The report was adopted. STOCKTON HEATH STREETS. Mr. Mclntyr3 raised the question of street cleaning in Stockton Heath. He said that one man had ceased work and no one hal ben appointed in his place. As a consequence, certain streets had been neglected. The burvo3or was instructed to report on the matter at the next meeting.

NEW POET LAUREATE. The King has been pleased to approve the appoint- ment of Mr. Robert Bridges, D. Litt, to be Poet Laureate. Robert Bridges is not so well known to the public as Mr. Rudyard Kipling, Mr. Thomas Hardy, or one or two of the other poets who were considered likely candidates for the Laureateship. This is owing to the more classical and esthetic nature of his work. A deeply-read scholar of the classics, much of his work is in the mould of classical models, and deals with classical subjects. Born in 1814, his life takes us back to the great poets of the Victorian era, whose successor he is by his admirers claimed to be. "The Growth of Love " and" Prometheus the Fire-Giver "are the most notable of his poems.
